## [2026-10-12] build | T-10400 regdiff tool
Built `tools/regdiff.py` and `tools/test_regdiff.py` (41 tests) on the T-9800 trace ([[uopt-register-allocation]], section "regdiff"). It compiles the permuter-stripped draft with `tools/cc.py` and the traced uopt, links register differences to live ranges through register webs of the draft's code and the trace's def/use counts, and prints the first divergence with priority, order, colour and a hint. Survey of the 163 best drafts of T-9800: no failure; traced compile 2.9 s average under load average 20 to 55, 1.4 to 2 s on a quieter machine. Usage: [[decompile-workflow]]. No compiler binary or reconstruction source added.

## [2026-10-12] decision | T-10400 rules from the tool's cases
Hoisted constants and addresses (bits past the expression table) are numbered by first use, left side of an assignment first; equal priority colours the lower bit first. A swapped `bne` with equal registers is operand order and a local copy of the narrow global fixes it. Two T-0018 rows matched with it: EVENT `func_80119518` (`u8 sel` copy) and DATE `func_80136F2C` (no temporary `v`), both through the real build (EVENT and DATE SHA1 OK). EVENT `func_80114B0C` draft improved to 5 differing lines (still guarded); TT `func_801475C8`, GYOZI `func_8013E638`, ETC `func_80143F24` explained, not matched. Notes in [[matching-notes]], rows in [[data/t0018-cases]].

## [2026-10-12] build | T-10400 sync_protos --add-global
`tools/sync_protos.py --add-global` dropped the `MAIN_API_OVERRIDE_<sym>` guards whose override is defined in a .c file and planned from the headers alone. It now plans from the declarations main_api.h holds plus the new symbol and keeps the existing guards, so exactly one line is added; test in `tools/test_sync_protos.py` (guards from a .c override survive, one line added, a following --write adds only what --write alone adds). The reported reordering by the following --write did not reproduce on a copy of the real include/ and src/ trees (only the guard loss did).

## [2026-10-14] build | T-11530 order_search.py added and validated

`tools/order_search.py` permutes marked statement blocks (`/* ORDER */ ... /* END ORDER */`) and operand orders
(`ORDER_SWAP(A, op, B)`), compiles every variant the way funcdiff does and scores it with resolved diff rows; the file
is always written back without markers. Recovered the matched store order of `func_80147150` from the pre-T-11500
order (24 variants, about 2 s) and the operand order of `func_80139770`. Two fixes during validation: plain threads
instead of `concurrent.futures` (`tools/queue.py` shadows the standard `queue` module) and NON_MATCHING only for
guarded functions, with `--resolve` as default. Synthetic tests `tools/test_order_search.py`, 10/10. Documented in
[[decompile-workflow]].

## [2026-10-14] build | T-11220 regdiff guidance in autotweak and bulk_m2c

`tools/autotweak.py`: with the traced uopt available, regdiff runs in-process on the start text and every new best
text; its FIRST DIVERGENCE (hint class, named variables and lines) orders the edits through one table,
`HINT_EDITS`, that follows [[register-cheatsheet]]. `--no-regdiff` turns it off; the output line ends with
`regdiff: <hint>`. `tools/bulk_m2c.py` passes it to the climb (`--no-regdiff`). Synthetic tests pass;
[[decompile-workflow]] updated. Needs the real-build comparison against `--no-regdiff`.

## [2026-10-14] build | T-11230 m2c variant sweep in bulk_m2c

`tools/bulk_m2c.py`: `--variants N` takes the first N option sets of `VARIANTS` (m2c 708d2d2 and `tools/m2c.py` options, checked with `m2c --help` in the toolchain image). It compiles and scores each one and keeps the lowest score (ties keep the earlier variant, 0 ends the sweep). The winner is recorded in state.jsonl and near.txt, and the summary counts wins per variant. `--retry near,fail [--max-score S]` processes those entries again and replaces their state lines. Synthetic tests pass; [[decompile-workflow]] updated. Needs the real-build trial.

## [2026-10-15] decision | T-16910 no cpw step; ticket to In Review
`cpw` does not meet CODING_STANDARDS 7a: its coupling selects one family, and the constant-first order it adds is an aggregate-member effect that C reading through the aggregate already gives (section 8). `tools/cvt_pass.py` unchanged; nothing matched. Open follow-ups: the type of GameState +0x1093/+0x1094, a census for the kept local copy, and the 5 `FAKE`/cast compares that could become aggregate reads.

[[tickets/T-16900-compiler-experiments]], item 4 of [[matching-breakthroughs]]. Scratch variant of `tools/cvt_pass.py`
(outside the repository) on the 7 frozen NAME_ENT drafts of [[tickets/T-11570-w1-ovl-name_ent-80139740]]. Widening
records (`CVT J<-J`, `CVT J<-L`) do not give the original's constant-first compare. Zcopy off plus constant first after

changes none: clean rebuild 27/27 sha1, and NAME_ENT.bin sha1 OK with the three drafts active (both via temporary,
reverted working-tree edits). It fires in no matched function, so the zero is not evidence for it. Meets the bar;

## [2026-10-16] query | T-19001 block-copy temporary rule

Measured the ugen temporary count of the 0x44-byte block copy in `func_80140A90` ([[tickets/T-19001-w3-d-p01]]) with about 80 variants of the real function and about 120 isolated `cc -S` contexts: one rotation step is skipped after a loop or a pointer store, two after a global or stack store, a call or at entry, and no code-neutral C changes the state. The original disagrees with that rule in SHUGAKU, MASTER and ENDING, so the gap looks like a ugen difference. ## [2026-10-16] build | T-19026 closer pass on TACO func_8015B1B8
Opus closer, worktree `w3-d-p26`, 16:22-16:45 UTC. `func_8015B1B8` stays guarded at 8 rows; nothing new matched. About 110 variants were scored with `cc -S` on a stand-alone copy of the function (scratch only). New finding: ugen puts `&matrix` in its own temporary `t2` before the loop, and as1 folds it away. Updated: the NON_MATCHING comment in `src/ovl/TACO/8015ABF0.c`, [[matching-notes]] (T-19026 closer pass), [[data/t0018-cases]], [[register-cheatsheet]] (struct-copy row), [[tickets/T-19026-w3-d-p26]] (stays In Review).

Claim #56: TT `func_8014D260`, the last TT function, through the gcc/maspsx path. Created [[tickets/T-11890-tt-ratan2-aspsx]]. First step is measuring the three ASPSX behaviours of [[gcc-objects]] over the SDK asm of the main exe before writing any rule.

## [2026-10-18] build | T-11890 baseline

Clean rebuild of branch `gcc-d260` (asm/ and build/ removed, configure.py, ninja): 27/27 SHA1 OK. A rebuild over the old
asm/ tree failed on stale NAME_ENT rodata paths; the clean split fixed it.

## [2026-10-18] query | T-11890 ASPSX habits over the SDK asm

New `tools/aspsx_survey.py` (counts only, no game bytes) over every `asm/lib*.s`. `mflo`/`mfhi` hazard nops: 290 of 290
sites follow one rule (pad before mult/div, a label or a gcc `.set noreorder` branch). `l* rd,sym(rs)`: 103 of 103 loads
with rd != rs go through rd; rd == rs and stores use `$at`. Divide move position: 63 of 67 sites fit "right after the
divide unless the checks read rd", 4 libspu `rem` sites do not, there is no SDK site with a signed quotient and rd
distinct, and maspsx's real-ASPSX fixture `div.yaml` contradicts it; ASPSX scheduled other instructions into 44 of the
67 expansions. Written to [[gcc-objects]] ("ASPSX habits in the SDK asm (T-11890)").

## [2026-10-18] decision | T-11890 no ASPSX pass for now

The divide-move habit has no uniform rule backed by the evidence, so no pass and no change to `tools/cc.py`,
`config/toolchains.txt` or `src/ovl/TT/8014D260.c` (CODING_STANDARDS 7a, claim #56 condition). The ratan2 C is settled
(draft in [[gcc-objects]]): with the three habits applied by hand to a scratch copy it gives all 97 instructions.
Next step proposed: run the real ASPSX binaries on test cases. Notes in [[tickets/T-11890-tt-ratan2-aspsx]],
[[matching-notes]], [[toolchain]]; the ticket stays In Progress.

## [2026-10-18] query | T-11890 real ASPSX on synthetic input

User-approved download of the maspsx author's `aspsx-binaries.tar.gz` into the scratchpad; ASPSX 2.56, 2.67, 2.77,
2.79, 2.81 and 2.86 run under wine in a throwaway container (nothing committed). 36 synthetic divide cases, hazard and
symbol-load cases, and the gcc 2.7.2-psx output of the ratan2 draft: all six versions agree with each other and with
maspsx (move after the checks, nop before a reader, no nop before labels or branches, symbol loads through `$at`;
ratan2 96 instructions against the original's 97). None of the three SDK habits is ASPSX behaviour. Results in
[[gcc-objects]] ("The real ASPSX on synthetic input (T-11890)"), [[tickets/T-11890-tt-ratan2-aspsx]], [[matching-notes]],
[[toolchain]].

## [2026-10-18] decision | T-11890 no ASPSX pass (confirmed by the real ASPSX)

An emulation pass would model behaviour the real assembler does not have, so none is built; `TT/8014D260` stays
INCLUDE_ASM and the ticket stays In Progress. The open lead is a compiler that emits the expanded divide and split
symbol loads itself.

## [2026-10-17] build | T-20011 source controls remain guarded

[[tickets/T-20011-name-ent-coordinate-selection]] preserves all default ASM fallbacks. Coordinate actor views reach13 aligned edits (bare-failure-exit diagnostic10); fade remains7; character page remains227. No C match claimed or promoted. Investigation also reproduces an independent search-ranking defect: funcdiff.diff_lines switches to positional rows only at equal instruction counts, while viewsweep and order_search use its printable row count as the score.
